<objective>
TPM and PIV CA key generations (D-14, KEY-08): the two hardware-backed backends learn what the software and agent backends learned in 03-12 and 03-12e, so the homelab's TPM signer can rotate its user CA and the PIV backend holds its generations without ever reusing a slot.

Purpose: 03-13 rotates the ops and log keys through the same allocator and the same fake card, and 03-15 runs `ca-rotate prepare --role user` on the homelab TPM signer before the v4 ceremony; both depend on this plan. Until it lands `ca-rotate prepare` and `retire-key` refuse tpm and piv by name (internal/signer/rotate.go:54-55).

Output:
- TPM generation files, generation addressing, presence checks and removal; PIV allocation by generation rows, addressing, presence checks and removal by overwrite; the fake card moved into a `pivfake`-tagged file behind an open hook;
- the backend gate lifted for tpm and piv, prepare naming the location it recorded, doctor's TPM check and TPM lost-prepare scan;
- the command-level restart, switch and removal legs on swtpm and on the fake card, run by new steps of the existing e2e-tpm and build-piv jobs with a PASS check per named test; TestTPMCARotation on swtpm;
- the per-backend runbook text (ca-rotation.md, signer-install.md, piv.md, custody.md, needs-hardware.md), the owed move-aside sentence before ceremony 1's export, and their pins;
- 03-12f2's early text and the one wave shift.
</objective>

## Where this plan stands

It is the plan of the CA-rotation set that 03-13 and 03-15 wait for (03-12f2 runs between it and 03-13 but changes nothing they use), planned out in full against main at 83f660c (2026-11-20: PR #112, which replaced lab names in planning text and two docs and changed no code, test, workflow or gate value since the merge of 03-12e2 as 2ed959f). Every anchor below was re-read at 83f660c ("Reconcile ledger (83f660c, 2026-11-20)"); Task 1's precondition stops if main moved in any file this plan reads, and every deviation found at execution goes in the SUMMARY.

**Stated plainly:** until this plan lands, a TPM or PIV signer cannot rotate a CA key: `ca-rotate prepare` and `retire-key` refuse both backends by name (internal/signer/rotate.go:54-55, called at cmd/keyroster-signer/rotate.go:79 and :178), and doctor reports any draining TPM or PIV generation as unchecked (cmd/keyroster-signer/doctor.go:230-231). After it lands, both backends rotate in CI only: on swtpm and on an in-memory fake card. **Every physical TPM, the homelab vTPM signer and every YubiKey stay UNVERIFIED** ("What stays UNVERIFIED").

## Owner decisions (final; do not ask again)

All answers are Daniel's.

- **The set (2026-11-13 and 2026-11-14).** Hardware is optional and never required: every path has a software or fake-card test. The draining key is removed from the backend right after the switch install. This plan lands before 03-13, which uses the PIV allocator, and before 03-15's prepare on the TPM homelab signer. Real PIV hardware stays UNVERIFIED. The scope, the test list (the TPM and PIV unit tests, TestSignerRestartMixedBundle on TPM and on the PIV fake card at command level, the e2e-tpm rotation test) and the mutants M175 to M179 are fixed. A rotation never changes the backend or the custody.
- **The host-CA runbook gate (the design record's option A for host-CA rotation, 2026-11-14).** Ceremony 2 for the host role is installed no earlier than ceremony 1's install plus the user profile's max TTL; automation and `--no-known-hosts` users run `known-hosts sync` first; the runbook says a client that never refreshes cannot be rotated safely. This binds 03-12f2, which carries it.
- **The rotation lane's time (2026-11-17).** If an `e2e-rotation` lane's `go test` takes more than 900 s, the executor stops and reports; no timeout is raised. This binds 03-12f2; this plan does not touch that lane's run list.
- **PIV removal (2026-11-20): overwrite on every firmware.** After the fingerprint check, retire-key overwrites the draining generation's slot with a throwaway key, on every firmware. Real deletion on firmware 5.7 and later is deferred until the pinned PIV library supports it and is recorded as a later hardening item. The fixed rule reads: never overwrite a slot, except the draining generation's own slot in retire-key. No dependency changes (T-03-SC: no module added). The owner was told the edge cases, and they stand: the first rotation overwrites a genesis slot; a lost response converges through Holds by fingerprint; whether a real overwrite destroys the old key is UNVERIFIED on hardware; the slot stays recorded and is never reused; the management key must be on the signer host for prepare and for retire-key.
- **The split (2026-11-20): two plans.** This plan keeps the TPM and PIV generations, the gate, doctor, the command-level and e2e-tpm proofs, the per-backend runbooks and the owed runbook sentence. 03-12f2 takes TestHostCARotation, its runbook text (the host-CA section of ca-rotation.md and the enroll-user.md paragraph), the e2e.yml lane edit and CONTRIBUTING. 03-13 and 03-15 depend on 03-12f only. ROADMAP goes from 43 to 44 plans and 03-13 to 03-16 shift one wave. This plan publishes 03-12f2's early text in its own PR, as 03-12e did for 03-12e2.
- **Local tests (2026-11-20).** The owner installs swtpm and libpcsclite-dev in WSL, so RED-first runs and mutants run locally through scripts/linux.sh; Task 1's precondition checks both and stops with a clear message if one is missing.
- **Delivery (2026-10-29 and 2026-11-02).** The plan lands in the same PR as its change, with its ROADMAP and STATE rows. Commits are signed and authored as Daniel, with keyroster-bot as committer, signer and pusher. The bot opens the PR without auto-merge, and Daniel squash-merges it himself.

## Open owner questions

None open. Every question this plan raised was answered on 2026-11-20 (above). The choices below are the planner's and change no owner decision.

## Planner decisions (Claude's discretion), recorded with their reasons

- **TPM Holds counts either file.** A TPM generation is held while `<role>[-g<N>].tpmkey` or `.auth` has any directory entry (regular file, damaged file, link, dangling link or directory); only the absence of both is "not held", and any other lstat error is "held" with the error. This is the fail-closed reading of the design record's "software and TPM: the generation's file exists (lstat; any entry counts)" (§3.12). It also makes retire-key's own re-check (internal/signer/rotate.go:558) catch a left-over auth file, so M179 dies on the real path and not only in the unit test.
- **TPM removal proves before it destroys, without the TPM.** RemoveGeneration reads the `.tpmkey` file only when it is a regular file, decodes the TSS2 PEM and compares the public key's fingerprint with the draining fingerprint (the public area is in the file; no TPM command, no auth). Another key refuses with `keystore.ErrGenerationFileMismatch` (its text "the file holds another key", internal/keystore/keystore.go:129, fits a key file). A file that does not decode refuses with a new `tpm.ErrKeyFileUnreadable`, because `keystore.ErrGenerationFileUndecryptable`'s text "the file cannot be decrypted" (:133) is wrong for a TSS2 PEM and keystore.go is not edited. Neither refusal removes anything. A link is unlinked, never followed. Any other entry at the `.tpmkey` name (a directory, a socket) is removed with `os.Remove`, as the software backend removes what is not a regular file (internal/keystore/software/software.go:550), which refuses a non-empty directory: then the error is returned, the entry stays and Holds answers held. So a regular key file is removed, together with its auth file, only after its public key matches the draining fingerprint; a link, another entry or a lone auth file at the generation's name is unlinked unchecked, because no key at that name can be opened. Order: `.auth` first, then `.tpmkey`, then one fsync of the key directory. This plan does not claim that one fsync orders the two unlinks: what keeps a crash fail-closed is the either-file Holds rule (serve refuses while either is left) plus a rerun of retire-key, which removes a lone `.auth` unchecked and checks a lone `.tpmkey` again.
- **TPM location is ignored.** A TPM row never carries a location (ProvisionGeneration returns `""`), and the file names derive from role and generation only, so an edited location cannot redirect a TPM key; KeyAt, Holds and RemoveGeneration ignore it, as the software backend does (internal/keystore/software/software.go:514).
- **TPM `--key` stays allowed, with noDA required from generation 2.** `prepare --key` on tpm opens a `<role>-g<N>.tpmkey` and `.auth` that the operator placed (parity with the software backend's placed file, docs/runbooks/signer-install.md:427-430). KeyAt refuses a generation-2-or-later key whose public area lacks noDA, so a placed key cannot bring back the TPM-wide dictionary-attack exposure that noDA removed (internal/keystore/tpm/provision.go:157-164); every key this backend creates has noDA, so the check costs nothing. Generation 1 is unchanged: keys created before noDA was set still open (internal/keystore/tpm/tpm.go:209-211). A planted key stays T-03-419's recorded residual (a logged leaf and a console fingerprint).
- **PIV location spelling.** The location of a PIV generation is the slot's id as two lowercase hex digits, `87` to `95`: the text piv-go's `Slot.String()` gives (key.go:464-466 at v2.6.0, `strconv.FormatUint(uint64(s.Key), 16)`). One strict parser accepts exactly those 15 strings; `0x87`, `8A`, ` 87`, `86` and `96` are refused. Generation 1 keeps the location `""` that ca-init writes (internal/signerdb/trust.go:96) and resolves to the role's genesis slot; generation 1 with any other location is refused; generation 2 or later with `""` is refused (never a fall back to the genesis slot: that is M177's and M356's behaviour).
- **PIV allocator.** The candidate is the first id in 0x87 to 0x95 that no entry of `used` names; every `used` entry must parse and none may repeat (a duplicate means the rows were edited, and the location column has no UNIQUE constraint, internal/signerdb/migrations/0006_ca_generations.sql:14), or the call is refused before any card command. No free id refuses `piv_no_free_slot` before any card command. An occupied candidate that no row records is refused with `ErrSlotOccupied` naming the slot: never skipped, which would burn a slot silently and would make the allocator go by occupancy, and never overwritten, which the owner's rule allows only in retire-key. Such a key arises from a lost prepare (the card generated the key and the row was not committed, internal/signer/rotate.go:235-244), from a signer.db restored from before a prepare, or from a key put there by hand. **The limit, kept on purpose and stated plainly in the runbooks and piv.md (Task 5):** the allocator makes the same choice on every later run, so that key blocks every later PIV prepare, for every role (and for 03-13's ops and log generations), until the slot is emptied. Overwriting the slot by hand does not clear it: the slot stays occupied, and the allocator refuses any occupied unrecorded candidate, whichever key it holds. Only emptying the slot clears it: on firmware 5.7 and later Yubico documents `ykman piv keys delete <slot>`, run only when the rule Task 5 writes into piv.md allows it (never on a restored database before its restore section has run to its end, never while a signed bundle waits to be installed, never a key kept there for another use); below 5.7 nothing empties a slot short of `ykman piv reset`, which destroys every PIV key on the card, the working CA keys included (firmware cannot be upgraded on a YubiKey). Every `ykman` command is UNVERIFIED here. Adopting an unrecorded generation key by its fingerprint, so that a lost prepare is recoverable on every firmware, is a later hardening item and not part of this plan. The owner was told this limit before dispatch.
- **PIV Holds and removal compare the key, never occupancy.** Holds reads the recorded slot's metadata under the mutex: `ErrNotFound` is not held, the draining fingerprint is held, another key is not held, any other error is held with the error, and a location that does not resolve is held with the error. After a throwaway overwrite the slot stays occupied, so Holds by occupancy would refuse serve for ever. RemoveGeneration reads the slot first: `ErrNotFound` is done with no write, another key refuses `ErrSlotHoldsAnotherKey` with no write, and only the draining fingerprint leads on to the management-key check and the throwaway `GenerateKey` (PIN once, touch never, the firmware's algorithm, internal/keystore/piv/piv.go:233-238); it then re-reads the slot and requires another key or `ErrNotFound`. This is the only write over an occupied slot, and it covers the genesis slots 0x82 to 0x84 when generation 1 drains, which is the first rotation's case. The scope is the recorded slot only, as the design record says (§3.12, "the recorded slot holds a key with that public key"); an edited location that points at any slot that does not hold the draining key (an empty slot, a later generation's slot, or a slot an earlier retire-key overwrote) makes Holds answer "not held" while the key stays in its real slot, a residual recorded as T-03-461. Holds does not scan the other slots of 0x82 to 0x95 for the draining key: a draining key is worth something only while a bundle still trusts it, and whoever can edit signer.db on the signer host can already read the PIN file and use the card, so 20 metadata reads at every start-up check would buy little; RemoveGeneration still writes only the recorded slot, and only after its key matched.
- **The PIV management key is read when it is used, never at open.** `parseOptions` keeps the `mgmt-key-file` path only (internal/keystore/piv/piv.go:192-209 reads and checks the file at open today). One helper, `managementKey()`, reads and checks the file with today's checks (a regular file, not a link; no group or other permissions; hex; 16, 24 or 32 bytes; the card's default management key refused) at the start of each operation that writes to the card, and before any card write: `Provision` (ca-init), `ProvisionGeneration`, and `RemoveGeneration` after its slot read, only when the slot holds the draining fingerprint. An option that is not set and a file that is not there both refuse with a text that names `mgmt-key-file`, and nothing is written. The reason is the operator's real state: ca-init stores the path, piv.md step 5 then shreds the file, and the shipped unit's ExecStart carries no override (deploy/systemd/keyroster-signer.service:26), so a backend that reads the file at open could not start serve, install-bundle or doctor on a signer that followed the runbook. With the read at use, every command that does not write to the card opens with the file absent and with no override, an empty override still works, and serve never holds the management key in memory, also when the file is present (the management key is used in `Provision` only today, piv.go:283-284 and :312, and piv.go's option comment at :31 already says it is needed only to generate keys). The key is read into a local value for the one operation and not kept in `config`. One effect is visible: a refused management-key file is now found after the PIN check at open, so ca-init with such a file verifies the PIN once before it refuses (a correct PIN spends no retry), where today's open refused it before sending the PIN; TestPIVRefusals's rule that an option refusal sends no PIN keeps covering the other options.
- **doctor does not warn about a management key left on the host (a later hardening item).** doctor could lstat the stored `mgmt-key-file` path of a PIV signer without a card, but the path lies below `/etc/keyroster-signer/piv`, which belongs to the signer's user, so the check needs a rule for a path doctor cannot read and a WARN-or-FAIL decision of its own; that is more than this plan's runbook fix needs, so the runbook's block (shred on every exit status, before the start) stays the control and the doctor check is recorded as a later hardening item.
- **RemoveGeneration's TPM key-file read is not hardened against a swap between its lstat and its read.** readPrivateFile lstats and then reads (internal/keystore/tpm/tpm.go:288-299), so a swap of the name between the two would make the fingerprint read follow a link. The swap needs write access to the 0700 key directory, which only the signer's user and root have; that user can already copy, replace or delete the key files, and the removal unlinks names and never follows a link, so the race gives no capability that the writer does not already hold. No change.
- **PIV `--key` is refused.** KeyAt refuses generation 2 or later with location `""`, and prepare's `--key` path passes `""` (internal/signer/rotate.go:192), so `prepare --key` on piv is refused (`signer: CA key selection refused`, internal/signer/trust.go:36) before anything is written and no slot is used; the help text says so. prepare itself is not changed for this.
- **prepare names the location it recorded.** cmd/keyroster-signer/rotate.go prints one more line, `location: <location>`, after `prepare file: <path>` when the row's location is not empty (today: only PIV), so the operator learns the slot from the console; software, agent and TPM output is unchanged (rotate.go:214-215).
- **The orphan message names the location.** `orphaned` (internal/signer/rotate.go:271-277) receives the location: a created key with a location is named with it (`at location <location>`); without one the message names both the software file and the two TPM files. No interface changes.
- **Doctor checks a TPM generation by its files.** drainingHeld gains `case name == "tpm":` and asks the new `tpm.GenerationHeld(stateDir, role, generation)`, which lstats the two names and sends no TPM command, as `software.GenerationHeld` needs no passphrase (cmd/keyroster-signer/doctor.go:245). The record allows it: "doctor opens the database read-only and no backend except to read a TPM" (03-12e-PLAN.md:1044), and the design record says Holds "is used by the start-up refusal, doctor and retire-key" (§3.12). PIV stays `WARN draining_key_unchecked`: asking the card costs a PIN verification per doctor run and competes with serve for the card's exclusive session. Doctor's lost-prepare scan (`unrecordedGenerationFiles`, doctor.go:302-329) also reads `{state-dir}/tpm` for `<role>-g<N>.tpmkey` and `.auth` with N of at least 2 that no row names: a TPM orphan is a working CA-capable key that the log never recorded.
- **The fake-card seam (needed by the fixed command-level PIV case and by 03-13).** The stored backend name must stay `piv`: CheckRotationBackend knows four names (internal/signer/rotate.go:44), doctor branches on the name, and a second registration panics (internal/keystore/registry.go:28-29). So piv.go gets one unexported hook, `var openCard = openYubiKey`, which `open` calls instead of `openYubiKey` (piv.go:116); a release build never assigns it. The fake card moves out of piv_test.go into a new non-test file `internal/keystore/piv/fake.go` tagged `//go:build piv && pivfake`, whose `init()` points `openCard` at a process-wide registry of fake cards, one per serial. piv_test.go's tag becomes `piv && pivfake` because its tests use the fake. The fake is our own code under a tag and adds no module; depguard applies to it (a non-test file below internal/keystore, .golangci.yml:42, :53), so it uses no os/exec. No existing piv test reaches the real opener (piv_test.go calls `newWithCard` only, through `openFake` at :223), so the hook changes no existing test. The exported test surface, which exists only in a `pivfake` build: `FakePIN`, `FakeManagementKeyHex` and `FakeCardGenerated(serial uint32) []uint32` (the slot ids the card generated into, in order).
- **The command-level legs keep the fixed names.** `TestSignerRestartMixedBundle/tpm` and `/piv` (cmd/keyroster-signer/rotate_test.go:406) each run the whole sequence: prepare, restart and issue with generation 1, ceremony 1's install and a restart, the switch install, serve's refusal, retire-key with its output and effects, a second retire-key, doctor, and a restart that issues with generation 2. The PIV leg's body lives in a new `rotate_piv_test.go` tagged `linux && piv && pivfake`, which sets a hook variable that rotate_test.go declares; without those tags the subtest skips with a message, and build-piv's PASS check turns a skip into a failure. The brief's separate TestRetireKey TPM and PIV legs are folded into these two legs: they need the same harness, and retire-key's output and effects are asserted there.
- **The command-level TPM harness is a `_test.go` copy of startSWTPM** (cmd/keyroster-signer/swtpm_test.go, `//go:build linux`), with the same `KEYROSTER_TPM_REQUIRE` guard, so e2e-tpm's new step runs before the job's shared swtpm starts and needs nothing from it.
- **The check helper of TestSignerRestartMixedBundle reads each row's location** from `db.CAGenerations` for every case (today it passes `""`, rotate_test.go:425, :435, :438). The software and agent backends ignore it, so their legs are controls; PIV needs it, or M177 hides.
- **The e2e-tpm rotation test is a new file** `test/e2e/tpm_rotation_test.go`, tagged `e2e_tpm` only, with helpers of its own names (`tpmRot...`), so ca_rotation_test.go (tag `e2e && e2erotation`, whose helpers 03-12f2 parameterises) stays untouched and no symbol is defined twice under combined tags. Its ceremony helper uses `keyrosterWithAgent` (test/e2e/bootstrap_test.go:393, built under `e2e_tpm`) where caRotCeremony uses `runKeyroster` (test/e2e/negative_test.go:252, tag `e2e` only).
- **CI reach without a new job.** The command-level TPM leg runs in a new step of the existing `e2e-tpm` job, the PIV leg in a new step of the existing `build-piv` job, each with a PASS-line loop per named test, and the existing unit steps gain PASS-line loops for the new unit tests. Where each proof runs: the TPM unit tests in e2e-tpm's unit step (swtpm required), TestSignerRestartMixedBundle/`tpm` in e2e-tpm's new step, TestTPMCARotation in e2e-tpm's e2e step; the PIV unit tests in build-piv's unit step and TestSignerRestartMixedBundle/`piv` in its new step; the gate and orphan cases of internal/signer, the doctor cases and the runbook pins need no device and run in the required `build-test` job (`CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -race -count=1 -timeout 15m ./...`, ci.yml:53), where the `tpm` leg skips (no swtpm) and the `piv` leg skips (no tags). No job is added or renamed, so the 30 required checks and the ruleset do not change and scripts/apply-rulesets.sh is not run.
- **CONTRIBUTING.md is not edited.** Its `e2e-tpm` and `build-piv` rows (:93-94) stay true after the new steps ("`ca-init --backend tpm` and the TPM keystore tests against swtpm"; "govulncheck, vet, lint, tests and build of the `-tags piv` YubiKey backend"), and the owner gave CONTRIBUTING's lane rows to 03-12f2. piv.md's "What CI verifies" lists the new steps instead.
- **The e2e.yml comment is re-pointed here, the lane is not.** The comment above the `e2e-rotation` job says that 03-12f adds TestHostCARotation (.github/workflows/e2e.yml:134-135); after the split that is 03-12f2's, so the comment names 03-12f2. Nothing else in e2e.yml changes.

## The output contract

These names and texts are fixed, byte for byte where tests match them.
- **TPM file names.** Generation 1 of a role: `{state-dir}/tpm/<role>.tpmkey` and `<role>.auth` (unchanged, internal/keystore/tpm/tpm.go:125-126). Generation N of at least 2: `{state-dir}/tpm/<role>-g<N>.tpmkey` and `<role>-g<N>.auth`, mode 0600, the auth 32 random bytes. Generation 0 is refused: `keystore tpm: no generation 0 of role <role>`. All five roles are accepted (tpm.go:121), so 03-13's ops and log generations work without a further keystore change.
- **TPM refusals.** ProvisionGeneration below generation 2: `keystore tpm: generation <N> of role <role> is not a later generation (generation 1 is ca-init's)`. An existing key or auth file: `tpm.ErrKeyFilesExist` (tpm.go:63) with the path and the hint `(after a restore, a <role>-g<N>.tpmkey or .auth file that no generation row names is a key from a lost prepare: once the restore section of docs/runbooks/signer-install.md has run to its end, destroy it unless you placed it for a --key prepare)` (the software backend's hint, internal/keystore/software/software.go:260, names no such condition and is not changed here: a recorded follow-up). A generation-2-or-later key without noDA: `keystore tpm: <path>: the key of generation <N> lacks noDA`. Removal of another key: `the file holds another key (<fingerprint>)` (keystore.ErrGenerationFileMismatch). A key file that does not decode: `tpm.ErrKeyFileUnreadable`, text `keystore tpm: the key file cannot be read as a TPM key`, wrapped with the path and the cause.
- **PIV.** Generation slots 0x87 to 0x95, 15 in all, shared by every role's generations 2 and later (03-13's ops and log too). Location text: two lowercase hex digits, `87` to `95`. Errors, each exported from package piv: `ErrNoFreeSlot` = `piv_no_free_slot`, used as `piv_no_free_slot: every generation slot from 0x87 to 0x95 is recorded by a generation row; nothing was generated`; `ErrBadLocation` = `keystore piv: the generation's location names no slot it may use`; `ErrSlotHoldsAnotherKey` = `keystore piv: the slot holds another key`; `ErrSlotOccupied` (piv.go:76) for an occupied unrecorded candidate, naming the slot and `nothing was generated`, then the pointer `; no generation row records the key in this slot, and every later PIV prepare stops here until the slot is emptied (docs/backends/piv.md)`. Without the management-key option: `keystore piv: creating a generation's key needs option mgmt-key-file (pass --backend-opt mgmt-key-file=FILE)` and `keystore piv: removing a generation's key needs option mgmt-key-file (pass --backend-opt mgmt-key-file=FILE)`; Provision keeps `keystore piv: provisioning needs option mgmt-key-file`. With the option set and its file missing or refused, each card write refuses with `keystore piv: mgmt-key-file <path>: <cause>`, the cause being today's check texts (the lstat error, `is not a regular file`, `want 0600 or stricter`, `must hold the management key in hex`, `want 16, 24 or 32`, `the card's default management key is refused`).
- **prepare.** Unchanged first two lines (`role <role> generation <N> <fingerprint> <alg> <custody>` and `prepare file: <path>`, cmd/keyroster-signer/rotate.go:214-215), then `location: <location>` only when the location is not empty. The `--key` help text (:145) reads `SHA256:... selects the new key in the backend (agent; software and tpm: a key file you placed; piv refuses it); without it the backend creates one (software, tpm, piv)`.
- **The gate.** `CheckRotationBackend` accepts agent, software, tpm and piv; any other name is refused `rotation_not_supported_on_backend: unknown backend "<name>"`. No error text names a plan.
- **retire-key** keeps its output form (cmd/keyroster-signer/rotate.go:106-135): TPM and PIV print `role <r> generation <g> <fingerprint>: removed`, `: not held` or `: not removed: <reason>`, then `retire-key: no draining key of role <r> is held` with exit 0, or `retire_key_incomplete` with exit 1.
- **The orphan message.** With a location: `(the backend created the key <fp> for generation <N> of <role> at location <location>, which no generation row records: remove it)`. Without: `(the backend created the key <fp> for generation <N> of <role>, which no generation row records: remove it; for the software backend it is {state-dir}/software/<role>-g<N>.age, for the tpm backend {state-dir}/tpm/<role>-g<N>.tpmkey and <role>-g<N>.auth)`.
- **doctor.** For tpm, as for software: `FAIL draining_key_still_usable: <role> generation <g> <fp> is still held by the backend` while either file is present, and no `draining_key_*` line after retire-key. For piv: `WARN draining_key_unchecked: ... doctor does not ask the piv backend ...`, unchanged. A TPM orphan: `WARN unrecorded_generation_file:` naming `{state-dir}/tpm/<role>-g<N>.tpmkey` and `.auth` as found. The warning's text is the shared one (internal/doctor/doctor.go:415), which says to destroy such a file after a restore and does not ask that the restore section has run to its end; signer-install.md states that condition (Task 5), and giving the shared text the condition is a recorded follow-up.
- **The runbook phrases the pins read** (Task 5 writes each word for word): in ca-rotation.md "1. Prepare the next key": `tpm/user-g2.tpmkey`, `tpm/user-g2.auth`, `0x87 to 0x95`, `15 slots`, `never reused`, `piv_no_free_slot`, `location:`, `line into the transcript`, `ErrSlotOccupied`, `slot already holds a key`, `no generation row records`, `ykman piv keys delete`, `never on a restored database`, `ykman piv reset`, `the block of piv.md step 5`, `after a refused prepare`, `no YubiKey or physical TPM has run it`; in "Remove the old key and restart", read as two narrower windows: from the line that starts `- **tpm:**` up to the line that starts `- **piv:**`, `tpm/user.tpmkey`, `tpm/user.auth`, `delete both files as the signer's user`, `no YubiKey or physical TPM has run it`; from the line that starts `- **piv:**` up to the line that starts `Start the signer only after retire-key exits 0`, `throwaway key`, `mgmt-key-file`, `never reused`, `the block of piv.md step 5`; the file holds no `rotation_not_supported_on_backend`; before every fenced block that writes `>/media/transfer/export.jsonl`, in the prose since the previous fenced block, the sentence that begins "First move `/media/transfer/export.jsonl` aside under another name and keep it" (the precedent, ca-rotation.md:493-496, which the existing check at cmd/keyroster/runbook_test.go:1175 reads in one section only). In signer-install.md "Prepare a CA key generation": `tpm/<role>-g<N>.tpmkey`, `0x87 to 0x95`, `piv_no_free_slot`, `mgmt-key-file`, `ErrSlotOccupied`, `slot already holds a key`, `no generation row records`, `ykman piv keys delete`, `never on a restored database`, `ykman piv reset`, `the block of piv.md step 5`, `after a refused prepare`; in "Install a successor bundle: stop, install, start", narrowed from the line that starts `### Remove the old key after a switch` up to the line that starts `Other refusals:`: `throwaway key`, `tpm/<role>.tpmkey`, `delete both files as the signer's user`; in the window of `### Restore from before the last switch` (its section, from that line up to the next `### ` or `## ` heading): `ls -la /var/lib/keyroster-signer/tpm/`, `a restore from before a prepare`. In docs/backends/piv.md, the window from the line `### 5. Remove the management key from the signer host` up to the line that starts `## Options` holds these five lines as five consecutive lines of one fenced block, each a whole line, in this order: `sudo systemctl stop keyroster-signer.service`, `K=/etc/keyroster-signer/piv/mgmt-key`, `sudo runuser -u keyroster-signer -- rm -f "$K"`, `sudo runuser -u keyroster-signer -- sh -c 'umask 077 && set -C && cat >"$1"' sh "$K"`, `sudo runuser -u keyroster-signer -- keyroster-signer ca-rotate <arguments> --state-dir /var/lib/keyroster-signer; rc=$?; sudo runuser -u keyroster-signer -- shred -u "$K"; test "$rc" -eq 0 && sudo systemctl start keyroster-signer.service`; and the phrases `on every exit status`, `only after the shred`, `copy-on-write`, `after a refused prepare`, `did not run to its end`; and it holds no line `sudo shred -u /etc/keyroster-signer/piv/mgmt-key`; every command of its fenced blocks that names `/etc/keyroster-signer/piv` or `$K` runs through `sudo runuser -u keyroster-signer` and, with that command's `sh -c` script masked, has no redirect that names either, the binding `K=/etc/keyroster-signer/piv/mgmt-key` excepted (the leaf's rule, Task 5). Every base value: 0 at 83f660c in its file (`grep -cF`, and also on whitespace-normalised text), except `umask 077` and `set -C`, which other blocks of the runbooks hold and which no pin counts, the old `sudo shred` line, which piv.md holds once (:219), and `never reused`, which ca-rotation.md holds once (:234, in "Abort", outside both windows that pin it). The negative pin on `rotation_not_supported_on_backend` reads a phrase that ca-rotation.md holds once at 83f660c (:489) and takes it to 0 by design.

## The fail-closed and liveness table (the one place the bypass and liveness checks read)

Kind **B** must refuse (or report held); kind **L** must still succeed. 03-12e's table covers the software and agent paths, serve's refusal and recovery; this table covers the TPM and PIV paths and the gate.

| Row | Kind | Path | Decision, and why | Case or control | Mutant |
|-----|------|------|-------------------|-----------------|--------|
| TP-01 | B | TPM KeyAt for generation N opens generation 1's file | refused: the file names derive from the generation; a fingerprint check alone does not stop `KeyAt(user,2,fp1)`, because `user.tpmkey` holds fp1 | TestTPMKeyAtExact/`generation_2_refuses_generation_1_fingerprint`; TestSignerRestartMixedBundle/`tpm` (the cross-check, rotate_test.go:434-440) | M178 |
| TP-02 | B | TPM KeyAt for a generation whose files are absent | refused, never a fall back to generation 1's files | TestTPMKeyAtExact/`missing_generation_no_fallback` | M354 |
| TP-03 | B | a generation-2-or-later TPM key without noDA (placed for `--key`) | refused at every open | TestTPMKeyAtExact/`noda_required_for_generation_n` | M353 |
| TP-04 | B | TPM Holds with only `.auth` left (a crash between the unlinks, or M179) | held | TestTPMHolds/`only_auth_held` | M340 |
| TP-05 | B | TPM Holds on an lstat error other than absence (the key directory replaced by a file) | held, with the error | TestTPMHolds/`lstat_error_held` | M355 |

## What stays UNVERIFIED, in the words the docs use

Task 5 writes this into ca-rotation.md "Run in CI", signer-install.md, piv.md, custody.md and needs-hardware.md, adapted to each place but without weakening it; the SUMMARY and the PR body repeat it.

> CI runs the TPM path on swtpm over a Unix socket (custody `vtpm`) and the PIV path on an in-memory fake card. Real hardware is not exercised. In particular:
> - **TPM:** `/dev/tpmrm0` and a physical TPM (custody `tpm`), their key creation, noDA against a physical TPM's dictionary-attack counter, and that the kernel's resource manager leaves no handle loaded (go-tpm-keyfiles' `FlushHandle` ignores errors). The homelab's vTPM signer: its first `ca-rotate prepare` is 03-15's and its first retire-key 03-16's. Removing `<role>[-g<N>].tpmkey` and `.auth` deletes the key's wrapped blobs; it destroys nothing inside the TPM. Any earlier copy of both files still signs on the same TPM until its owner hierarchy is cleared: a VM snapshot, a hypervisor backup that holds the vTPM state, a hand copy of the state directory, and unerased blocks on an SSD or a copy-on-write filesystem.
> - **PIV, on every YubiKey:** generating into 0x87 to 0x95; reading a slot's metadata when it is empty, overwritten or never used; that an overwrite is atomic and destroys the old private key (Yubico's documented behaviour, not observed here); signing from a generation slot after a reopen in a separate process; the algorithm per firmware for generations; PIN once across processes; the card's answer to a wrong management key; every `ykman` command the runbook names, `ykman piv keys delete <slot>` and `ykman piv reset` included; and the 15-slot exhaustion on a real card, which CI checks on the fake card only. Deletion on firmware 5.7 and later is not implemented: the pinned piv-go v2.6.0 has no delete command, so retire-key overwrites on every firmware.
> - **A key in a generation slot that no generation row records stops PIV rotation.** A lost prepare, a signer.db restored from before a prepare, or a key put there by hand leaves a key in the slot the allocator picks next; every later PIV prepare, for every role, then refuses `ErrSlotOccupied` until the slot is emptied. Overwriting the slot by hand does not clear it. On firmware 5.7 and later `ykman piv keys delete <slot>` empties it, when the rule of piv.md "Generation slots" allows it (never on a restored database before its restore section has run to its end, never while a signed bundle waits to be installed, never a key kept there for another use); below 5.7 nothing empties a slot short of `ykman piv reset`, which destroys every CA key on the card.
> - **The management key file is shredded, not proven erased.** `shred` does not reliably erase a file on an SSD or a copy-on-write filesystem.
> - **The location column is not logged.** A database edit of a PIV generation's location is stopped by the fingerprint checks before any card write, but an edit that points at any slot that does not hold the draining key makes Holds answer "not held", and audit cannot show which slot a generation used.

## Budget

files_modified lists 32 files: 19 code, test, e2e and workflow files (3 in internal/keystore/tpm, 3 in internal/keystore/piv with fake.go new, 2 in internal/signer, 6 in cmd/keyroster-signer with rotate_piv_test.go and swtpm_test.go new, cmd/keyroster/runbook_test.go, test/e2e/tpm_rotation_test.go new, and e2e-tpm.yml, piv.yml and e2e.yml), 5 docs (ca-rotation.md, signer-install.md, piv.md, custody.md, needs-hardware.md) and 8 planning files (this plan, 03-12f2-PLAN.md new, the four later plans' frontmatter, ROADMAP and STATE). The SUMMARY is the one file the branch adds beyond the list.

Per task (files touched, tokens raw, by the method of 03-12e2-PLAN.md's Budget: each task session reads the shared sections of this plan, about 30k tokens, plus its own task, the code it changes and its runs; mutant records cost about 3k to 4k each): Task 1 3 files, 115k (the TPM backend, 37 RED lines, 9 mutants on swtpm); Task 2 4 files (piv.yml in S2), 180k (the seam move, the release guard and its recorded negative proof, the management key read at use, the backend, 34 RED lines, 14 mutants); Task 3 10 files, 160k (the gate, the two command-level legs and their harnesses, the PIV leg with the management-key file absent outside prepare and retire-key, doctor, two workflows, 6 RED runs (four with FAIL lines, two controls), 3 mutants and the 4 command-level re-runs of M177 to M179 and M363); Task 4 2 files, 75k (the e2e test and a local run on swtpm); Task 5 7 files, 150k (five docs with piv.md's management-key block and its generation-slot section, the block leaf's redirect rule and its five fixtures, the e2e.yml comment, their pins, the restore runbook check, 5 mutants); Task 6 8 planning files, 190k (it reads this plan's own staged diff in full, about 75k tokens, and 03-12f2's, the bookkeeping diffs, the staged-line scans, the CI lane gates and the SUMMARY); Task 7 none, 5k. **That is about 875k raw at low confidence** (no calibration samples, so the projection is not scaled; comparable runs went 2x to 5x higher).

**This is above the design record's cut line of about 280k (§8.3) by about 3.1 times, and above 03-12e2's 420k.** The owner chose this two-way split on 2026-11-20 with the planning brief's figure of about 650k raw for this half in front of him, so this plan does not cut further and raises no split. If the orchestrator wants it smaller before dispatch, the one cut that saves most without repeating work is **PIV and the fake-card seam as a plan of their own** (Task 2, the PIV leg and the piv.yml steps of Task 3, the PIV text of Task 5, about 300k raw): PIV carries the cross-plan contract with 03-13 (the fake card at command level, 03-13-PLAN.md:154) and the heaviest new CI surface, while the TPM half is what 03-15 needs. That cut is not made here: it would put a third gate state on main (tpm accepted, piv still refused) and a second pass over the same runbook windows, and it needs the owner's word, because he decided the split.

**Test time (Q-09-3, owner decision, 03-09-PLAN.md).** cmd/keyroster (runbook pins), cmd/keyroster-signer (the two legs, doctor), internal/signer (the gate and orphan cases), internal/keystore/tpm (swtpm) and internal/keystore/piv (the fake) change in a way that costs test time. Last readings: 03-12e's base `keyroster=292.275 signercmd=40.709 signer=429.150` (03-12e-SUMMARY.md:35), 03-12e2's `keyroster=291.128` (03-12e2-SUMMARY.md:41), and in CI internal/keystore/tpm 1.577 s and internal/keystore/piv 1.077 s on main at 2ed959f (E2E TPM run 44661168798, PIV run 44661168902). These orient the budget; the gate is the delta over the base this plan measures.
- **Timeout.** Every go test command carries `-timeout 15m`, every mutant command `-timeout 5m`. A timeout is not a kill.
- **Base.** Task 1 measures a quiet base on unchanged origin/main, before any edit and with no other heavy job running, one package at a time: `bash scripts/linux.sh 'CGO_ENABLED=1 KEYROSTER_TPM_REQUIRE=1 go test -race -count=1 -p 1 -timeout 15m ./cmd/keyroster/ ./cmd/keyroster-signer/ ./internal/signer/ ./internal/keystore/tpm/ && CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -race -count=1 -timeout 15m -tags piv,pivfake ./internal/keystore/piv/'` (on main `pivfake` is an unknown tag that changes nothing, so the same command runs at the base and on the branch). It records exactly one SUMMARY line, `test-time base: keyroster=<s> signercmd=<s> signer=<s> tpm=<s> piv=<s> at=<epoch seconds>`, each time read from the package's `ok` line without the trailing `s`. The command takes about 13 minutes (the readings below add up to about 760 s before compiling), longer than a tool's foreground limit of 10 minutes, so it runs in the background (`run_in_background`) and its output is read when it ends; the same holds for Task 6's delta gate and full gate.
- **The rule.** Task 6's delta gate runs the same command and stops if any of the five packages adds more than 90 s to its base. A base more than 24 h old, or measured in another session or WSL boot, is re-measured on origin/main in a detached worktree outside the repository, immediately before; a re-measure REPLACES the base line in place. Expected: keyroster +2 to +5 s, signercmd +20 to +60 s (the TPM leg runs locally, because swtpm is installed; build-test, which has no swtpm, skips it), signer +1 to +3 s, tpm +5 to +20 s, piv +1 to +3 s. A reading above +60 s is a warning recorded with the cases that cost most.
- **Whole module.** `go test -race -count=1 -timeout 15m ./...` is pass or fail only and is never compared with a base.
- **The CI lanes this plan changes.** e2e-tpm took about 2 min 7 s on main at 2ed959f (job 136039490748: the unit step 15 s, the e2e step 33 s) and build-piv about 2 min 8 s; the new steps add the command-level legs (tens of seconds under `-race`) and TestTPMCARotation (seconds: it waits for no drain and no host). e2e-tpm's e2e step has no `-timeout`, so Go's default of 10 minutes applies. The 900 s stop rule binds the `e2e-rotation` lane only, which this plan does not change. The main e2e lanes are unchanged (they compile neither `e2e_tpm` nor the `piv` tag).
- **Ruleset.** The ruleset lists 30 required checks (`.github/rulesets/main-integrity.json`, CONTRIBUTING.md:63); this plan adds no job and renames none (the new steps live in the existing `e2e-tpm` and `build-piv` jobs), so `scripts/apply-rulesets.sh` is not needed and is not run.

## Phase Goal

> Enrolled Linux hosts are kept in sync automatically (trusted CAs, principals, KRL and host certificates), so access granted, revoked or rotated centrally takes effect across the fleet without hand-edited sshd config and without risking lockout. (ROADMAP Goal, verbatim.)

  <behavior>
    - Generation N of at least 2 of a role is created inside the TPM with noDA as `tpm/<role>-g<N>.tpmkey` and `<role>-g<N>.auth` (mode 0600, the auth 32 bytes), and generation 1's files keep their names; KeyAt opens exactly the generation's two files, refuses every other fingerprint, never falls back to another generation, refuses a generation-2-or-later key without noDA and ignores the location.
    - Holds is held while either file has any directory entry, not held only when both are absent, and held with an error on any other lstat error, a generation 0 or an unknown role; GenerationHeld answers the same from a state directory without a TPM.
    - RemoveGeneration removes `.auth` and then a regular `.tpmkey` only after the key file's public key matches the fingerprint, unlinks a link without following it, removes an empty directory at the key name and refuses a non-empty one, removes a lone `.auth` unchecked, fsyncs the key directory once, and returns nil when both are absent; another key or a file that does not decode refuses and removes nothing.
  </behavior>

R1 declares, on `*backend` in tpm.go and provision.go, with production behaviour unchanged: `KeyAt(role, generation, fingerprint, location)` (generation 1 returns `b.Key(role, fingerprint)`, every other generation an unexported `errNotYet`), `ProvisionGeneration(role, generation, used)` (`nil, "", errNotYet`), `Holds(...)` (`true, errNotYet`) and `RemoveGeneration(...)` (`errNotYet`); the package function `GenerationHeld(stateDir, role, generation)` (`true, errNotYet`); and the variable `ErrKeyFileUnreadable` with the contract's text. No compile-time assertion yet. The methods already make the backend satisfy the four interfaces, so `keystore.KeyFor` reaches the stub KeyAt at generation 1, which delegates to Key exactly, and `keystore.Holds` reaches the stub Holds, which answers held as before (keystore.go:166-170 answered held with `ErrHoldsUnsupported`); generation 2 is unreachable while the gate refuses tpm (internal/signer/rotate.go:54-55). The SUMMARY lists these declarations.
The tests, in tpm_test.go. Each top-level test starts its own swtpm with `startSWTPM` and opens no backend itself; each leaf makes its own state directory, opens one backend, provisions the five genesis keys with `provision`, and closes every backend before it opens the next (swtpm serves one connection at a time). Every refusal leaf first runs its positive control. The leaves:
- `TestTPMGenerationFiles`: `names_and_modes` (after `ProvisionGeneration(user, 2, nil)` and `ProvisionGeneration(ops, 2, nil)`: `user.tpmkey` and `user.auth` byte-identical to before, `user-g2.tpmkey`, `user-g2.auth`, `ops-g2.tpmkey` and `ops-g2.auth` regular files with mode 0600, each auth `authSize` bytes, the returned location `""` and an `ecdsa-sha2-nistp256` key; the files of every other role byte-identical), `noda_on_generation_key` (`user-g2.tpmkey` decoded as TestProvisionNoDA reads a key file: noDA, fixedTPM, fixedParent, sensitiveDataOrigin, userWithAuth and sign set).
- `TestTPMProvisionGenerationRefusals`: `generation_below_2_refused` (0 and 1 refused naming `generation 1 is ca-init's`, nothing written; positive: 2 succeeds), `existing_key_file_refused` and `existing_auth_file_refused` (a file at the generation's key or auth name: `ErrKeyFilesExist` with the lost-prepare hint, the existing bytes unchanged and the other name not created; positive: generation 3 succeeds), `key_dir_not_private_refused` (the key directory at 0750: refused naming `want 0700`, nothing written; positive after the mode is restored), `symlinked_key_dir_refused` (the key directory replaced by a link to a 0700 directory: refused naming `is not a directory`, nothing written in either directory), `sync_failure_leaves_nothing` (the `syncDir` hook fails for the key directory: refused naming the sync, and no `user-g2.*` file is left; positive with the hook restored).
- `TestTPMKeyAtExact` (after Provision and `ProvisionGeneration(user, 2, nil)`, fp1 and fp2 the two user keys): `generation_2_signs` (KeyAt(user, 2, fp2, "") signs and verifies, custody vtpm), `generation_2_refuses_generation_1_fingerprint` (positive: KeyAt(user, 2, fp2); then KeyAt(user, 2, fp1) refused although `user.tpmkey` holds fp1: the M178 case), `generation_1_refuses_generation_2_fingerprint`, `works_with_generation_1_removed` (`user.tpmkey` and `user.auth` deleted by the test: generation 2 still opens and signs), `after_reopen` (close, reopen on the same swtpm and state, both generations open by number and fingerprint), `missing_generation_no_fallback` (positive: KeyAt(user, 2, fp2); then KeyAt(user, 3, fp1) refused: no generation 3 files exist and generation 1's are never tried), `noda_required_for_generation_n` (positive: the noDA key of generation 2 opens; then a generation-3 key that the test creates with tpm2.Create and keyTemplate's attributes minus noDA, written as `user-g3.tpmkey` and `user-g3.auth`, is refused naming `lacks noDA`), `location_ignored` (KeyAt(user, 2, fp2, "87") opens the same key as with "").
- `TestTPMHolds`: `absent_not_held` (generation 3, never created: `false, nil`), `regular_file_held`, `dangling_link_held`, `link_held` (a link to another file), `directory_held` (a directory at the name), `only_auth_held` (only `user-g2.auth` left: held; the M340 case), `only_tpmkey_held`, `lstat_error_held` (positive: `false, nil` for generation 3; then the key directory replaced by a regular file: `true` with a non-nil error), `bad_generation_or_role_held` (positive as above; then generation 0 and role `foo`: `true` with an error), `generation_held_matches_holds` (for absent, present and only-auth states, `GenerationHeld(stateDir, ...)` returns what the backend's Holds returns; for the absent state both are `false, nil`). Every held leaf of an entry that exists (`regular_file_held`, `dangling_link_held`, `link_held`, `directory_held`, `only_auth_held`, `only_tpmkey_held`) also asserts `err == nil`; `lstat_error_held` and `bad_generation_or_role_held` assert a non-nil error.
- `TestTPMRemoveGeneration`: `tpm_retire_removes_both_files` (fixed name: RemoveGeneration(user, 1, fp1, "") leaves neither `user.tpmkey` nor `user.auth`; the `syncDir` hook records exactly one call, for the key directory; Holds(user, 1) is `false, nil`; the other roles' files and `user-g2.*` byte-identical; a second call returns nil; the M179 case), `other_key_refused` (positive: the right fingerprint removes generation 2 in a second state; then `user-g2.tpmkey` and `.auth` replaced by generation 3's files: `ErrGenerationFileMismatch` naming the other fingerprint, both files still there), `unparsable_key_file_refused` (garbage at `user-g2.tpmkey`: `ErrKeyFileUnreadable`, both files still there), `link_unlinked_target_intact` (`user-g2.tpmkey` a link to a file outside the key directory: the link and `user-g2.auth` are gone and the target is byte-identical; in a second state an empty directory at `user-g2.tpmkey` is removed with `user-g2.auth` and Holds is `false, nil`; in a third, a directory holding a file at that name: an error is returned, the directory and its file stay and Holds answers held), `crash_states_converge` (only `user-g2.auth` left: removed unchecked; only `user-g2.tpmkey` left: checked, then removed; Holds `false, nil` after each; then a lone `user-g2.tpmkey` holding generation 3's key: `ErrGenerationFileMismatch` and the file stays), `sync_failure_returned` (the `syncDir` hook fails: the error names the sync).

- tpm.go: `keyPathsAt(role, generation)` (the role check of :120-124 first; generation 0 refused; 1 gives `<role>.tpmkey` and `<role>.auth`; N of at least 2 gives `fmt.Sprintf("%s-g%d", role, N)` plus the two extensions), and `keyPaths(role)` becomes `keyPathsAt(role, 1)`. `KeyAt` takes Key's whole body (:135-196) on `keyPathsAt(role, generation)`, with error texts that name the generation, and, for generation 2 or later, refuses a public area without noDA after `checkGeneratedInTPM`; it ignores location. `Key(role, fp)` becomes `b.KeyAt(role, 1, fp, "")`, as software.go:429-431, so ops and log, which open through `Key` (internal/signer/signer.go:296), are unchanged. `Holds` lstats both names and sends no TPM command (serve's check opens no key, internal/signer/trust.go:602); `GenerationHeld(stateDir, role, generation)` builds a backend value with only the key directory, as software.go:490-492. `RemoveGeneration` per the planner decision (the private directory first, then lstat of both names; `.tpmkey` read through `readPrivateFile` only when it is a regular file, decoded with `keyfile.Decode`, its `PublicKey()` compared by `ssh.FingerprintSHA256`; `.auth` removed first, then `.tpmkey`, then `syncDir(b.dir)`). The four assertions `var _ keystore.GenerationBackend = (*backend)(nil)` and the same for `GenerationProvisioner`, `GenerationHolder` and `GenerationRemover`, so that a lost method is a build failure instead of a silent fall back to Key (KeyFor, keystore.go:111-116). The package doc names the generation files, the removal and what it does not destroy.
- provision.go: `ProvisionGeneration(role, generation, _ []string)`: refuse below 2; `checkPrivateDir(b.dir)` with no mkdir (ca-init made it); refuse when either name exists (lstat), with the contract's hint; then 32 random bytes of auth, `createKey` under `tpmMu` with `keyTemplate()`, the same P-256 and non-empty PEM checks as Provision, `writeNew` of the key and then the auth, then `syncDir(b.dir)`; on any failure remove what was written; return `pub, "", nil`. One helper, shared with Provision, creates one key and checks it, so noDA cannot drift between the two; it adds no `syncDir` call to Provision (TestProvisionSyncsDirectories counts them).

  <behavior>
    - In a `pivfake` build, `keystore.Open("piv", ...)` opens an in-memory fake card per serial, which refuses a second open while it is open; a build without `pivfake` contains no fake and opens a real card through piv-go.
    - ProvisionGeneration takes the first slot in 0x87 to 0x95 that no `used` entry names, refuses `piv_no_free_slot`, a malformed or duplicate entry, an occupied unrecorded candidate and a missing management key before any card write, and returns the slot as two lowercase hex digits; ops and log draw on the same pool.
    - KeyAt, Holds and RemoveGeneration resolve generation 1 with `""` to the role's genesis slot and generation 2 or later only to a canonical 0x87 to 0x95 location; Holds and RemoveGeneration compare the key in the slot, never its occupancy; RemoveGeneration overwrites only the slot that holds the draining key, with a throwaway key, and converges when the card acted but the answer was lost.
    - The backend opens, and signs, with the stored `mgmt-key-file` path naming a missing file or a file it would refuse, and never reads the file at open; Provision, ProvisionGeneration and RemoveGeneration read and check it at the start of their card write and refuse a missing or refused file, naming `mgmt-key-file`, with no card write.
  </behavior>
  <action>
**Refactor first: commit S2** (`refactor(keystore): move the PIV fake card into a pivfake-tagged file behind an open hook`). No behaviour of a build without `pivfake` changes.
- piv.go: one unexported package variable `openCard` (initially `openYubiKey`), documented as the only seam a `pivfake` build repoints; `open` calls `openCard(cfg.serial)` where it called `openYubiKey(cfg.serial)` (:116).
- fake.go (new, first line `//go:build piv && pivfake`): the fake card moved byte for byte from piv_test.go (`testPIN` and `testMgmtKey`, :35-40, which the fake's constructor uses; `allRoles`, in the same `var (` block at :41, stays in piv_test.go; `fakeSlot`, `fakeCard` and its methods, `fakePINRetries`, `newFakeCard`, `fakeSigner`, `newKey`, `publicEqual`, :44-197), its comment saying it is test code compiled only with `pivfake`; and a process-wide registry of fake cards, one per serial (serial 0 is the card of an option without `serial`), created on first use at firmware 5.7.0, guarded by its own mutex. `openFakeCard(serial)` returns a session on the card and refuses, with `fake card <serial> is in use by another session`, while an earlier session of that card is still open, as a PC/SC transaction does; the session's Close ends the session, logs the card out (as the fake's Close does, :159-163) and releases the card. An `init()` sets `openCard = openFakeCard`. Exported, and present only in a `pivfake` build: `FakePIN` (equal to `testPIN`) and `FakeManagementKeyHex` (the hex of `testMgmtKey`), so a command-level test writes the pin and management-key files the fake accepts. The fake card itself keeps no lock, so the race detector still catches unserialised backend access (piv_test.go:50-56).
- piv_test.go: the moved declarations are deleted (the test helpers `writeSecret`, `validOpts`, `openFake`, `buildRequest`, `issue` and `allRoles` stay), and its build line becomes `//go:build piv && pivfake`, because its tests use the fake.
- piv.yml, in the same commit so the lane stays green: the vet step vets both tag sets (`-tags piv` and `-tags piv,pivfake`, each over `./internal/keystore/piv/... ./cmd/keyroster-signer/...`); the lint step's `--build-tags` becomes `piv,pivfake` (a superset of the release files); the unit step runs `go test -race -tags piv,pivfake -count=1 -timeout 15m -v ./internal/keystore/piv/...`, writes its output through `tee` to a log under `$RUNNER_TEMP`, so the job log shows it too, and checks a `--- PASS: <name> (` line for each of TestPIVProvisionAndIssue, TestPIVRefusals, TestPIVPINRetriesGuard, TestPIVSignWithoutMgmtKey and TestPIVConcurrency (with `-tags piv` alone the package would now report no test files and pass silently, which the loop refuses); and **the release guard**, appended to the `run:` block of the step `Build keyroster-signer with the PIV backend` (:70-75), whose four existing lines stay byte-identical; the step gains no other line and no key (no `if:`, no `continue-on-error:`), so its text from its name to the next step's name is the 7 lines of 83f660c plus the guard. The guard sits between two comment lines inside that block, `# release piv guard: begin` and `# release piv guard: end` (each exactly once in piv.yml; 0 at 83f660c), and takes the form piv.yml already uses at :80-91. It holds, in this order: two variable lines, `tags=piv` and `bin="$RUNNER_TEMP/keyroster-signer-piv"`; the plain line `go tool nm "$bin" >"$RUNNER_TEMP/piv-syms.txt"`; five checks, each written `if <test>; then echo "release piv guard: <which check>" >&2; exit 1; fi` over one or more lines: (1a) the positive control, `go list -tags "$tags,pivfake" -f '{{join .GoFiles " "}}' ./internal/keystore/piv` through `tr ' ' '\n'` does not hold the line `fake.go` (`grep -Fqx`); (1) the same with `-tags "$tags"` holds it; (2) the build-info line of `"$bin"` whose second tab-separated field is `build` and whose third begins `-tags=` is not exactly the literal `-tags=piv` (`go version -m` prints each build setting as a tab, `build`, a tab and `key=value`, the go command joining the tags with commas); (3a) the positive control, the symbol file lacks `internal/keystore/piv.(*backend).Key` (`grep -qF`); (3) the symbol file holds a symbol of package piv whose name contains `fake` in any case; then the one line `echo "release piv binary: -tags=piv, no fake.go, no fake symbol"`. No line of piv.yml begins with `!` after its indent: the job runs `bash -eo pipefail` (`shell: bash`, :33-35), where a `!` line whose pipeline succeeds does not stop the step, so a guard written that way could never fail; no check ends in `|| true`. The `uses:` lines, the triggers, the permissions and the default-build step (:76-93) do not change.
Run the S2 gate (the third verify command), read the staged diff of every file in full, and commit S2.

**The release guard's negative proof (the mutant rule for a CI guard), right after S2 and before R2.** In WSL through scripts/linux.sh, with `RUNNER_TEMP` set to a fresh `mktemp -d` outside the repository: extract the guard as CI runs it, the lines strictly between the two marker lines with the block's ten-space indent removed (`sed -n '/# release piv guard: begin$/,/# release piv guard: end$/p' .github/workflows/piv.yml | sed '1d;$d' | sed 's/^          //'`), into a file outside the repository, and cut it at its `if` and `fi` lines into its parts (the two variable lines, the nm line, the five checks, the echo). Then run under `bash -eo pipefail`, each time with the two variable lines replaced: leg 1, check (1) alone with `tags=piv,pivfake`; leg 2, check (2) alone with `bin` set to a binary built by `CGO_ENABLED=1 go build -tags piv,pivfake -o "$RUNNER_TEMP/fake-signer" ./cmd/keyroster-signer`; leg 3, the nm line and check (3) alone with `bin` set to that same binary. Each leg must exit non-zero and print its own `release piv guard:` line on stderr. The two positive controls, (1a) and (3a), are not run on a tripping input; the correct run below shows them passing. Then the step's whole `run:` body (the four unchanged lines and the guard, with the original variable lines), extracted as `sed -n '/^      - name: Build keyroster-signer with the PIV backend$/,/^      - name: Default builds exclude piv-go and cgo$/p' .github/workflows/piv.yml | sed '1,2d;$d' | sed 's/^          //'` (the step's name and `run: |` lines and the next step's name dropped), must exit 0 and print `release piv binary: -tags=piv, no fake.go, no fake symbol`. The SUMMARY records the four exit codes and each leg's stderr line. A leg that exits 0 means the guard cannot fail: fix the guard and amend S2 before R2. The success line in a CI log is never evidence that the guard can fail.

**RED first: commit R2** (`test(keystore): pin the PIV generation slots, presence and removal`). R2 declares in piv.go, with production behaviour unchanged: `KeyAt` (generation 1 with location `""` returns `b.Key(role, fingerprint)`, everything else an unexported `errNotYet`), `ProvisionGeneration` (`nil, "", errNotYet`), `Holds` (`true, errNotYet`), `RemoveGeneration` (`errNotYet`), and the exported errors `ErrNoFreeSlot`, `ErrBadLocation` and `ErrSlotHoldsAnotherKey` with the contract's texts; in fake.go: per-slot GenerateKey failure injection with a mode that replaces the slot's key and then fails (a response lost after the card acted), a log of the slot ids the card generated into, a `generateAttempts` counter incremented at the top of GenerateKey before its management-key check, and the exported `FakeCardGenerated(serial uint32) []uint32`, which returns a copy of a registered card's log. KeyFor at generation 1 reaches the stub and delegates to Key exactly; generation 2 is unreachable while the gate refuses piv. The SUMMARY lists these declarations.
The tests, in piv_test.go, on fake cards made with `newFakeCard` and opened with `openFake` (each leaf its own card, 5.7.0 unless a leaf names another firmware), every refusal leaf with its positive control first:
- `TestPIVGenerationAllocator`: `first_generation_slot_is_87` (`ProvisionGeneration(user, 2, nil)` returns location `87`; slot 0x87 holds a key generated on the card with PIN once and touch never; the generate log ends in 0x87), `piv_deleted_slot_not_reused` (fixed name: after generation 2 took `87`, the test empties slot 0x87 by deleting it from the fake, as an operator's `ykman` delete would; `ProvisionGeneration(user, 3, ["87"])` returns `88` and never touches 0x87; an emptied slot is what an occupancy allocator would choose, so this leaf and not an overwrite kills M175), `piv_no_free_slot_refused` (fixed name: positive with 14 of the 15 recorded, which takes `95`; then with all 15 recorded: `ErrNoFreeSlot`, `generateAttempts` unchanged, every slot unchanged), `never_overwrites` (fixed name: positive with `["87"]`, which takes `88`; then on a fresh card whose slot 0x87 holds a key that no row records: `ErrSlotOccupied` naming slot `87` and `nothing was generated`, with the pointer's `until the slot is emptied` and `(docs/backends/piv.md)`, the slot's key unchanged, no generate attempt; a second call with the same `used` refuses the same way), `used_malformed_refused` (positive `["87"]`; then `["0x87"]`, `["8A"]`, `["86"]`, `["96"]` and `["87", "87"]` each refused before any card call), `without_mgmt_key_refused` (positive with the key; then a backend opened without `mgmt-key-file`: refused naming `mgmt-key-file`, no generate attempt), `generation_below_2_refused` (positive first: `ProvisionGeneration(user, 2, nil)` returns `87`; then 0 and 1 are refused and `generateAttempts` is unchanged), `ops_and_log_allocate_from_the_same_pool` (`ProvisionGeneration(ops, 2, ["87"])` returns `88`, then `(log, 2, ["87", "88"])` returns `89`), `algorithm_by_firmware` (5.4.3 generates `ecdsa-sha2-nistp256`, 5.7.0 `ssh-ed25519`).
- `TestGenerationKeyAfterReopen` (fixed name): its body provisions the genesis keys and generation 2 of user (location loc2), closes the backend and reopens it with `newWithCard` on the same fake, so on R2 the body fails before the leaves start; the leaves, which first run on T2: `generation_2_by_its_slot` (KeyAt(user, 2, fp2, loc2) signs), `empty_location_refused` (positive as above; then KeyAt(user, 2, fp2, "") and KeyAt(user, 2, fp1, "") refused: the second is the M356 case, because the genesis slot holds fp1), `genesis_key_not_at_generation_slot` (KeyAt(user, 2, fp1, loc2) refused), `generation_1_with_a_location_refused` (KeyAt(user, 1, fp1, "") signs; KeyAt(user, 1, fp1, "82") refused).
- `TestPIVGenerationSlots`: `location_range_refused` (positive: KeyAt, Holds and RemoveGeneration of generation 2 at `87` and at `95` do not return `ErrBadLocation`; then `82` to `86`, `96`, `0x87`, `8A`, ` 87` and `""` for generation 2 return `ErrBadLocation` from all three, with no card write), `generation_1_takes_no_location` (generation 1 with `82` returns `ErrBadLocation`; with `""` it opens).
- `TestPIVHolds` (each leaf builds its own fake card and backend, and the parent body holds no setup, so that on R2 each leaf fails in its own setup or assertion; its first four leaves on generation 2 of user at loc2, `87`, after the genesis keys and `ProvisionGeneration(user, 2, nil)`, so that the genesis slot 0x82 holds fp1 and only the recorded slot holds fp2): `held_by_fingerprint` (Holds(user, 2, fp2, loc2) is `true, nil`; the M366 case, because the genesis slot holds another key), `throwaway_not_held` (another key generated into the recorded slot: `false, nil`; the M343 case, because the slot stays occupied), `empty_slot_not_held` (the recorded slot emptied: `false, nil`), `read_error_held` (positive `held_by_fingerprint`'s state; then an injected KeyInfo failure of the recorded slot: `true` with that error), `bad_location_held_with_error` (`86` for generation 2: `true` with `ErrBadLocation`), `genesis_slot_for_generation_1` (Holds(user, 1, fp1, "") is `true, nil`).
- `TestPIVRemoveGeneration`: `overwrites_the_genesis_slot` (RemoveGeneration(user, 1, fp1, ""): slot 0x82 now holds another key generated on the card with PIN once and touch never and the firmware's algorithm; the generate log ends in 0x82; Holds(user, 1, fp1, "") is `false, nil`; every other slot unchanged; the first rotation's case), `overwrites_a_generation_slot` (generation 2 at loc2, the same assertions), `other_key_refused` (positive in a second backend; then the recorded slot holds another key: `ErrSlotHoldsAnotherKey`, no generate attempt, the slot unchanged), `empty_slot_writes_nothing` (the recorded slot emptied: nil, no generate attempt; the M357 case), `without_mgmt_key_refused` (positive with the key; then a backend without `mgmt-key-file`: refused naming `mgmt-key-file` and `--backend-opt`, no generate attempt, the slot still holds the draining key), `half_finished_overwrite_converges` (the injected replace-then-fail: RemoveGeneration returns an error, and Holds is then `false, nil` and a second RemoveGeneration refuses `ErrSlotHoldsAnotherKey` with no write: retire-key's next run prints `not held`), `bad_location_refused` (`86` for generation 2: `ErrBadLocation`, no card call).
- `TestPIVManagementKeyAtUse` (new; each leaf builds its own fake card, files and backend, and the parent body holds no setup, so that on R2 each leaf fails on its own; R2 also deletes the three cases `mgmt_key_file_mode_0640_refused`, `default_mgmt_key_refused` and `mgmt_key_not_hex_refused` from TestPIVRefusals's open-time table, whose other cases stay, because their checks move to first use and are asserted here): `open_with_the_file_missing` (the options name a `mgmt-key-file` path that does not exist: `newWithCard` succeeds after one PIN verification, `Key(user, fp1)` signs, and `Provision`'s refusal on a fresh card names `mgmt-key-file` with `GenerateKey` never called), `open_does_not_read_the_file` (a file at that path with mode 0640, then in a second backend one holding the card's default management key: each opens and signs, so open read neither), `missing_file_refused_at_each_write` (positive first: with a valid 0600 file, `Provision` on a fresh card, `ProvisionGeneration(user, 2, nil)` and `RemoveGeneration(user, 1, fp1, "")` each succeed in a backend of its own; then, in fresh states, with the file removed after open: each of the three refuses with a text that begins `keystore piv: mgmt-key-file ` and names the path, `generateAttempts` unchanged, every slot unchanged, and for RemoveGeneration the slot still holds the draining key), `invalid_file_refused_at_each_write` (positive as above; then the file replaced after open by one with mode 0640, a link to a valid file, one that is not hex, one of 20 bytes and one holding the card's default management key: each of the three operations refuses naming `mgmt-key-file` and today's cause, `want 0600 or stricter`, `not a regular file`, `hex`, `want 16, 24 or 32` and `default management key`, with `generateAttempts` unchanged; and a well-formed wrong key, which the helper accepts: the fake card refuses it at GenerateKey and every slot is unchanged).
- `TestPIVFakeRegistry` (a control on R2: S2 and R2's fake declarations make it pass): `keystore.Open("piv", ...)` with a 0600 pin file holding `FakePIN`, a management-key file holding `FakeManagementKeyHex` and a serial of its own reaches the fake (its Describe names firmware 5.7.0); a second `keystore.Open` of that serial while the first is open is refused naming the card in use; after Close a reopen works; after Provision `FakeCardGenerated` lists 0x82 to 0x86 in order.
Run `git diff --cached` on every staged file and read all of it, then commit R2, then run the lint passes. Take the RED from a worktree of R2 (the second verify command). Expected `--- FAIL:` lines (34): TestPIVGenerationAllocator and its 9 leaves (10); TestGenerationKeyAfterReopen (1: its leaves never start on R2); TestPIVGenerationSlots and its 2 leaves (3); TestPIVHolds and its 6 leaves (7); TestPIVRemoveGeneration and its 7 leaves (8); TestPIVManagementKeyAtUse and its 4 leaves (5: on R2 the open still reads the file, and the stubs refuse the positive controls). Expected to pass on R2, as controls: TestPIVFakeRegistry and every existing piv test (TestPIVRefusals without the three moved cases).

**GREEN: commit T2** (`feat(keystore): allocate, open, check and remove PIV CA key generations`). T2 changes no test and not fake.go. piv.go: the location parser (exactly the 15 strings `fmt.Sprintf("%x", id)` for id 0x87 to 0x95) and the resolver `generationSlot(role, generation, location)` per the planner decision, both refusing with `ErrBadLocation`; `keyInSlot(role, slot, fingerprint)` holding Key's body (:339-377 after the slot lookup: origin generated, fingerprint, `cert.CheckCAKey`, the locked signer), so that `Key(role, fp)` stays the genesis-slot path that ops and log use and `KeyAt` resolves its slot and calls `keyInSlot`; the management key read at use, per the planner decision (`config` keeps the path as `mgmtKeyFile string` in place of `mgmtKey []byte`, `parseOptions` reads no file, and `managementKey()` reads and checks it with today's checks and texts, wrapped as `keystore piv: mgmt-key-file <path>: <cause>`; `Provision` calls it where it checked `b.cfg.mgmtKey == nil`, keeping that text for an unset option; the key is a local value of the one call and is never stored in `config`; piv.go's option comment (:31) and the package doc say that the file is read only when a key is generated or overwritten, by ca-init, prepare and retire-key, and that every other command opens without it); `ProvisionGeneration` per the planner decision (refuse below 2 and without the management-key option before any card call; every `used` entry parsed strictly, a duplicate refused; the first unrecorded id; `ErrNoFreeSlot`; under the mutex `occupied(candidate)`: an occupied candidate refused `ErrSlotOccupied` with the contract's pointer after `nothing was generated` (the pointer goes on this branch only; `orphaned()` in internal/signer keeps its generic text), and an unreadable candidate refused with occupied()'s own `keystore piv: read slot <s>: <err>`, unchanged and with no pointer, because no row-less key is known to be there; in both cases nothing generated; then `managementKey()`, refusing before any card write; then `GenerateKey` with the firmware's algorithm, PIN once and touch never; the `pub.Type()` check of Provision; returns the location); `Holds` and `RemoveGeneration` per the planner decision, both under the mutex, RemoveGeneration calling `managementKey()` only after its slot read found the draining fingerprint and before its `GenerateKey`, its throwaway public key discarded; the four compile-time assertions; the package doc gains the generation slots, the location, the no-reuse rule, the one exception to "never overwrite" and the management key, and keeps "What CI does not verify" true; `ErrSlotOccupied`'s comment (:74-75) names the exception. card.go, yubikey.go and go.mod do not change.
Run `git diff --cached` on every staged file and read all of it, then commit T2. Then run the commands below.

Commands: `bash scripts/linux.sh 'CGO_ENABLED=1 go vet -tags piv ./internal/keystore/piv/... ./cmd/keyroster-signer/... && CGO_ENABLED=1 go vet -tags piv,pivfake ./internal/keystore/piv/... ./cmd/keyroster-signer/... && test -z "$(gofmt -l .)" && CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -race -count=1 -timeout 15m -tags piv,pivfake ./internal/keystore/piv/ && CGO_ENABLED=1 $HOME/go/bin/golangci-lint run --build-tags piv,pivfake ./internal/keystore/piv/... && $HOME/go/bin/golangci-lint run ./... && GOOS=windows $HOME/go/bin/golangci-lint run ./... && GOOS=darwin $HOME/go/bin/golangci-lint run ./... && bash scripts/testonly-firewall.sh && bash scripts/dep-firewall.sh'`.

**The mutants of this task**, each with `bash scripts/linux.sh 'CGO_ENABLED=1 go vet -tags piv,pivfake ./internal/keystore/piv/ && CGO_ENABLED=1 go test -count=1 -timeout 5m -tags piv,pivfake -v -run "^(TestPIVGenerationAllocator|TestGenerationKeyAfterReopen|TestPIVGenerationSlots|TestPIVHolds|TestPIVRemoveGeneration|TestPIVManagementKeyAtUse)$" ./internal/keystore/piv/'`: M175 (the candidate is the first slot from 0x87 that KeyInfo reports empty), M176 in two applications of one idea, recorded as M176a (the occupied-candidate refusal removed: ProvisionGeneration generates over the key) and M176b (with every slot recorded, the allocator takes 0x95 instead of refusing), M177 (KeyAt uses `slotFor(role)` for every generation), M343 (Holds answers held for any key in the slot), M344 (Holds answers `false, nil` on a KeyInfo error other than `ErrNotFound`), M345 (RemoveGeneration skips the fingerprint comparison), M346 (the resolver accepts 0x82 to 0x86 for generation 2 or later), M347 (the allocator skips an entry of `used` that does not parse, and a duplicate), M348 (RemoveGeneration skips its option check and `managementKey()`, so it calls GenerateKey with a nil key), M356 (generation 2 or later with location `""` resolves to `slotFor(role)`), M357 (RemoveGeneration's early return on `ErrNotFound` removed, so it generates a throwaway into an empty slot), M363 (`parseOptions` reads and checks the `mgmt-key-file` file at open again, as on main, and stores the key in `config`), M364 (ProvisionGeneration goes on when `managementKey()` returns an error, calling GenerateKey with the nil key it returned), M366 (Holds validates the location and then reads `slotFor(role)` for every generation).

  <done>A PIV signer allocates each CA key generation a slot that no generation row records and never reuses it, opens a generation only at its recorded slot, tells whether the draining key is still in that slot by the key itself, and overwrites the draining slot, and nothing else, with a throwaway key; it reads the management key only for a card write, so a signer whose key is off the host opens, serves and installs bundles; a release piv binary contains no fake card, and build-piv proves it with a guard whose every check was shown to fail on a fake-bearing input.</done>

<task type="auto" tdd="true">
  <name>Task 3: The gate, prepare and retire-key on tpm and piv at command level, doctor's TPM check, and the CI steps that run them</name>
  <precondition>T2 is committed on the branch, and after `git fetch origin` Task 1's stop guard and base-value checks still pass on origin/main. Otherwise stop and report for a re-reconcile.   <behavior>
    - CheckRotationBackend accepts agent, software, tpm and piv and refuses any other name without naming a plan; prepare prints the location it recorded when there is one; the orphan message names the location, or the software and TPM files when there is none.
    - On a TPM signer (swtpm) and on a PIV signer (the fake card), at command level: prepare creates generation 2 (no `--key`; on PIV `--key` is refused and uses no slot); the signer restarts and issues with generation 1 before the switch; after the switch install serve refuses `draining_key_still_usable`; retire-key removes the old key (TPM: both files; PIV: the overwrite of slot 0x82, refused first without the management key) and a second run reports `not held`; the signer restarts and issues with generation 2. The PIV leg keeps the management-key file at its stored path only for prepare and retire-key, and every other command runs with it absent and with no override.
    - doctor fails `draining_key_still_usable` while a draining TPM generation's file is present and is silent after its removal, warns about TPM generation files that no row records, and keeps `WARN draining_key_unchecked` for PIV.
    - The e2e-tpm and build-piv jobs run the two legs and the new unit tests, and fail when one of them did not report a PASS line.
  </behavior>
